- **Score**: 7/10

### FairTranslate: An English-French Dataset for Gender Bias Evaluation in Machine Translation by Overcoming Gender Binarity
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.15941v1)
- **Authors**: Fanny Jourdan, Yannick Chevalier, Cécile Favre
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly leveraged for translation tasks but often fall short when translating inclusive language -- such as texts containing the singular 'they' pronoun or otherwise reflecting fair linguistic protocols. Because these challenges span both computational and societal domains, it is imperative to critically evaluate how well LLMs handle inclusive translation with a well-founded framework. This paper presents FairTranslate, a novel, fully human-annotated dataset designed to evaluate non-binary gender biases in machine translation systems from English to French. FairTranslate includes 2418 English-French sentence pairs related to occupations, annotated with rich metadata such as the stereotypical alignment of the occupation, grammatical gender indicator ambiguity, and the ground-truth gender label (male, female, or inclusive). We evaluate four leading LLMs (Gemma2-2B, Mistral-7B, Llama3.1-8B, Llama3.3-70B) on this dataset under different prompting procedures. Our results reveal substantial biases in gender representation across LLMs, highlighting persistent challenges in achieving equitable outcomes in machine translation. These findings underscore the need for focused strategies and interventions aimed at ensuring fair and inclusive language usage in LLM-based translation systems. We make the FairTranslate dataset publicly available on Hugging Face, and disclose the code for all experiments on GitHub.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "FairTranslate: An English-French Dataset for Gender Bias Evaluation in Machine Translation by Overcoming Gender Binarity":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FairTranslate, a new English-French dataset designed to evaluate gender bias in machine translation (MT) systems, specifically addressing both binary and non-binary gender representations.  The dataset contains English-French sentence pairs related to occupations, annotated with detailed metadata including stereotypical alignment of the occupation, grammatical gender ambiguity, and gender labels (male, female, inclusive). The authors evaluate four leading LLMs (Gemma2, Mistral, Llama3.1, Llama3.3) on the dataset using various prompting strategies (task prompting, moral prompting, linguistic prompting, combined).  Results show substantial gender biases across LLMs, especially with inclusive language, and highlight the need for strategies to ensure fairer and more inclusive translation. The dataset and code are made publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The most significant contribution is the creation of the FairTranslate dataset. While prior work addresses gender bias in MT, FairTranslate's explicit focus on non-binary gender representations and the rich metadata provided for each sentence pair sets it apart. The annotation with stereotype association and ambiguity is valuable.  However, the general methodology of evaluating LLMs with a new dataset and different prompting strategies is not entirely novel in itself. Similar evaluations have been conducted in other bias-related areas within NLP. The prompts used are also standard, drawn from other work. The novel element comes from the combination of the dataset and evaluation specifically targeting a multilingual non-binary bias scenario.

* **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial issue: the perpetuation of societal biases, particularly related to gender, in widely used MT systems. By focusing on both binary and non-binary aspects, the research highlights the need for more nuanced and inclusive MT models. The public release of the dataset will likely spur further research and development in this area.  However, the scope is limited to English-French translation.  While this is a well-motivated choice, the generalizability of the findings to other language pairs (particularly those with different grammatical gender systems or cultural contexts) might be limited.  Furthermore, while the results clearly demonstrate the existence of biases, the analysis doesn't delve deeply into *why* these biases exist, beyond suggesting a lack of moral and linguistic awareness. More investigation into the model architectures or training data used might provide more actionable insights. Also, the paper does not explore techniques to *mitigate* the bias only to evaluate the models for it.

* **Strengths:**
    *  Well-constructed and richly annotated dataset.
    *  Clear and comprehensive experimental setup.
    *  Rigorous evaluation using multiple metrics and prompting strategies.
    *  Publicly available dataset and code, facilitating further research.
    *  Addresses a very timely and important issue.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * Limited language pair (English-French).
    * Lack of in-depth analysis into the underlying causes of the biases.
    * Focuses on evaluation, with no concrete methods to mitigate biases shown.
    * Some methodological aspects (prompting strategies) are not highly novel.

* **Potential Influence:** The dataset will likely be adopted by other researchers working on fairness and bias in MT. It provides a valuable benchmark for evaluating and improving the performance of MT systems with respect to gender inclusivity. The clear demonstration of the persistent biases, even with prompting interventions, will hopefully encourage the development of more robust and inclusive MT models. However, its impact will depend on how widely the dataset is adopted and whether researchers build upon this work to develop bias mitigation techniques.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:** The FairTranslate dataset is a significant contribution to the field, addressing the critical issue of non-binary gender bias in machine translation. The thorough annotation and well-executed evaluation provide valuable insights. However, the limited language pair, lack of bias mitigation strategies, and lack of detailed analysis prevent it from achieving a higher score. While it builds on existing methodologies, the specific focus on the non-binary gender dimension in MT and the curated dataset warrants a solid "7," indicating a valuable and above-average contribution with reasonable impact and potential influence.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Adversarial Observations in Weather Forecasting
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.15942v1)
- **Authors**: Erik Imgrund, Thorsten Eisenhofer, Konrad Rieck
- **Abstract**: AI-based systems, such as Google's GenCast, have recently redefined the state of the art in weather forecasting, offering more accurate and timely predictions of both everyday weather and extreme events. While these systems are on the verge of replacing traditional meteorological methods, they also introduce new vulnerabilities into the forecasting process. In this paper, we investigate this threat and present a novel attack on autoregressive diffusion models, such as those used in GenCast, capable of manipulating weather forecasts and fabricating extreme events, including hurricanes, heat waves, and intense rainfall. The attack introduces subtle perturbations into weather observations that are statistically indistinguishable from natural noise and change less than 0.1% of the measurements - comparable to tampering with data from a single meteorological satellite. As modern forecasting integrates data from nearly a hundred satellites and many other sources operated by different countries, our findings highlight a critical security risk with the potential to cause large-scale disruptions and undermine public trust in weather prediction.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel attack targeting AI-based weather forecasting systems, specifically autoregressive diffusion models like Google's GenCast. The attack aims to manipulate weather forecasts by injecting subtle, adversarial observations into the input data, fabricating extreme events or concealing real ones. The authors propose a new algorithm that approximates the inference process of diffusion models to generate effective perturbations. This algorithm addresses the challenges of attacking autoregressive diffusion models, which are difficult to target directly due to their iterative denoising process. The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of their attack through empirical evaluation, showing that even small changes to weather observations (comparable to data from a single satellite) can significantly alter forecast predictions. The paper also explores the limitations of statistical detection as a countermeasure. The authors conclude that AI-based weather forecasting systems are vulnerable to manipulation and that robust data security and model evaluation are crucial before widespread deployment.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper is highly novel. This appears to be the first dedicated work rigorously analyzing adversarial attacks against AI weather forecasting, and specifically targeting the leading edge architectures based on diffusion models. The approximation algorithm for targeting autoregressive diffusion models in this domain is a notable technical contribution. It correctly addresses the challenges that come from trying to attack these models via standard methods and proposes a novel approximation of the inference procedure that balances the need to include both small and large noise levels to stabilize optimization. The idea of injecting small changes to sensor readings that remain statistically similar to natural noise is also novel.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance is substantial. The increasing reliance on AI in weather forecasting, coupled with the inherent vulnerabilities introduced by decentralized data sources, creates a real and pressing security risk. The paper effectively demonstrates this risk and highlights the potential for malicious actors to cause disruption or undermine public trust in weather prediction. This is also timely, since more and more governments are beginning to adopt AI approaches into their traditional workflows. The authors also show that standard detection mechanisms are insufficient, which raises immediate concerns and should spark much discussion in the field. The specific extreme weather predictions that are easily fabricated (extreme rainfall, hurricanes, heat waves) make this work high-stakes.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies and articulates a novel security threat.
    *   **Technically Sound Approach:** The proposed attack algorithm is well-reasoned and addresses specific challenges of attacking diffusion models.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The empirical evaluation is thorough, covering different geographic locations, time periods, and target variables. It also analyzes multiple baselines and ablation studies. The inclusion of real events like Hurricane Katrina is compelling.
    *   **Realistic Threat Model:** The authors consider realistic constraints on the attacker's capabilities and the inherent noise in weather observations.
    *   **Responsible Disclosure:** The authors follow responsible disclosure practices.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **White-box Assumption:** The assumption of white-box access to the forecasting model might be limiting, but the authors appropriately argue this is currently a reasonable assumption. Furthermore, the assumption may not hold true in the future as security concerns rise.
    *   **Limited Scope of Countermeasures:** The paper primarily focuses on statistical detection and concludes it's not viable, but more exploration of alternative defense mechanisms could be beneficial. Broader suggestions for trusted data source and robustness are given, but these could be fleshed out further.

*   **Potential Impact:** This paper is likely to have a significant impact. It should spur further research on adversarial robustness in weather forecasting and prompt stakeholders to consider security implications when deploying AI-based systems. It will raise awareness of the risks to both meteorologists and the wider ML security community.

**Score: 9**

**Rationale:**

The paper makes a substantial contribution by identifying and demonstrating a previously unaddressed security vulnerability in a critical infrastructure domain (weather forecasting). The proposed attack is technically novel and effective, and the empirical evaluation is compelling. While the white-box assumption is a limitation, the authors adequately justify it in the current context. The potential impact on the field is high, as it highlights the need for greater security awareness and robust defenses in AI-based weather forecasting systems. Only a few minor limitations are found, and the contribution is generally well presented, timely, and impactful. The work is poised to change the conversation around the reliability of AI forecasts, making it an exceptional contribution.
- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 9/10

### Universal Approximation with Softmax Attention
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.15956v1)
- **Authors**: Jerry Yao-Chieh Hu, Hude Liu, Hong-Yu Chen, Weimin Wu, Han Liu
- **Abstract**: We prove that with linear transformations, both (i) two-layer self-attention and (ii) one-layer self-attention followed by a softmax function are universal approximators for continuous sequence-to-sequence functions on compact domains. Our main technique is a new interpolation-based method for analyzing attention's internal mechanism. This leads to our key insight: self-attention is able to approximate a generalized version of ReLU to arbitrary precision, and hence subsumes many known universal approximators. Building on these, we show that two-layer multi-head attention alone suffices as a sequence-to-sequence universal approximator. In contrast, prior works rely on feed-forward networks to establish universal approximation in Transformers. Furthermore, we extend our techniques to show that, (softmax-)attention-only layers are capable of approximating various statistical models in-context. We believe these techniques hold independent interest.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper proves the universal approximation capabilities of softmax attention mechanisms in sequence-to-sequence tasks. The authors demonstrate that both two-layer self-attention and one-layer self-attention followed by a softmax function (with only linear transformations) can approximate any continuous sequence-to-sequence function on a compact domain.  They introduce a new interpolation-based method for analyzing attention's internal mechanism, showing that self-attention can approximate a generalized ReLU function, and hence encompasses many known universal approximators.  They also show attention-only layers can approximate statistical models in-context.  Unlike previous works that rely on deep attention stacks or feed-forward networks, this paper highlights the expressive power derived solely from the attention module itself.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its demonstration that *attention alone*, without auxiliary feed-forward networks or deep stacks, is sufficient for universal approximation. Prior works had not isolated the attention mechanism in this way, often relying on other components to achieve universality. The interpolation-based analysis method is also a significant contribution, providing a new way to understand how attention works internally and how it relates to known universal approximators. The extension to in-context learning, showcasing attention's ability to mimic gradient descent, is another strong addition.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work is two-fold. First, it provides a deeper understanding of the fundamental expressive power of attention mechanisms. It simplifies the understanding of Transformers and may lead to more efficient architectures. Second, the theoretical results may have practical implications, suggesting that attention-only models could be effective in scenarios where computational resources are limited or where interpretability is crucial. Furthermore, demonstrating the ability of attention alone to achieve gradient-descent-like learning within the in-context setting connects Transformers to more traditional statistical learning models and could inform the design of more sample-efficient meta-learning algorithms.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Strong Theoretical Results:**  The proofs appear technically sound (though a full verification would require detailed review). The theorems are clearly stated, and the assumptions are minimal (compactness of the domain).
    *   **Clear and Well-Structured:** The paper is well-organized, with a clear explanation of the key ideas and contributions.
    *   **Novel Analysis Technique:**  The interpolation-based method provides a new way to analyze attention mechanisms.
    *   **Practical Relevance:** By demonstrating the expressive capabilities of simple attention mechanisms, the work could inspire the design of more efficient and interpretable models.
    *   **Connections to Existing Work:** The authors thoroughly relate their work to existing literature and clearly point out how it differs and improves upon previous approaches.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Empirical Validation:** While the paper includes numerical experiments, they are primarily proof-of-concept and on synthetic data. The real-world impact remains to be seen. It will be important to show that these theoretical results can translate to improvements on complex, real-world tasks using attention-only architectures.
    *   **Constant mappings only:** The universal approximation proofs are valid for continuous *constant* mappings only, not for mappings from time-series data.
    *   **Practical Considerations of Implementation:** The theory doesn't necessarily translate easily into practical architectures. The size of weight matrices derived from this theorem might be larger than conventional attention stacks that rely on more layers to facilitate universality.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper is likely to influence future research on attention mechanisms and Transformers. It may lead to a re-evaluation of the role of feed-forward networks in these architectures, and inspire new approaches to model design. The analysis techniques could also be valuable for understanding other neural network architectures.

**Overall Score and Justification:**

I assign this paper a score of **8/10**.

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a significant and novel theoretical contribution to our understanding of attention mechanisms. Its demonstration of universal approximation with attention *alone* is an important breakthrough, distinct from and complementary to the existing works. The analysis is theoretically sound, but the limited empirical validation on simple synthetic tasks is an important weakness, and so this pushes the score down a bit. While having interesting theoretical implications, the theorem does not seem to easily translate to practical large language model settings with time-series data and complex datasets, which is another reason for the downgrade. But overall, the results presented are compelling and significant enough to warrant a high score of 8.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 0/10

### FreeGraftor: Training-Free Cross-Image Feature Grafting for Subject-Driven Text-to-Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.15958v1)
- **Authors**: Zebin Yao, Lei Ren, Huixing Jiang, Chen Wei, Xiaojie Wang, Ruifan Li, Fangxiang Feng
- **Abstract**: Subject-driven image generation aims to synthesize novel scenes that faithfully preserve subject identity from reference images while adhering to textual guidance, yet existing methods struggle with a critical trade-off between fidelity and efficiency. Tuning-based approaches rely on time-consuming and resource-intensive subject-specific optimization, while zero-shot methods fail to maintain adequate subject consistency. In this work, we propose FreeGraftor, a training-free framework that addresses these limitations through cross-image feature grafting. Specifically, FreeGraftor employs semantic matching and position-constrained attention fusion to transfer visual details from reference subjects to the generated image. Additionally, our framework incorporates a novel noise initialization strategy to preserve geometry priors of reference subjects for robust feature matching. Extensive qualitative and quantitative experiments demonstrate that our method enables precise subject identity transfer while maintaining text-aligned scene synthesis. Without requiring model fine-tuning or additional training, FreeGraftor significantly outperforms existing zero-shot and training-free approaches in both subject fidelity and text alignment. Furthermore, our framework can seamlessly extend to multi-subject generation, making it practical for real-world deployment. Our code is available at https://github.com/Nihukat/FreeGraftor.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FreeGraftor, a novel training-free framework for subject-driven text-to-image generation. It aims to address the fidelity-efficiency trade-off prevalent in existing methods. FreeGraftor leverages cross-image feature grafting, specifically employing semantic matching and position-constrained attention fusion to transfer visual details from reference subjects to the generated image. A key component is a novel noise initialization strategy to preserve the geometric priors of the reference subjects, enhancing feature matching.  The authors demonstrate through experiments that their approach achieves precise subject identity transfer while maintaining text alignment, outperforming existing zero-shot and training-free approaches.  Furthermore, the method can be extended to multi-subject generation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using cross-image feature grafting in the feature space of a MM-DiT model for subject-driven generation has some novelty. The combination of semantic matching, position-constrained attention fusion, and structure-consistent noise initialization appears to be a unique approach. While appearance transfer has been explored, the specific application and combination of techniques for *identity preservation* in MM-DiT architecture makes it unique. The noise initialization, in particular, appears to be a well-considered addition. However, the individual components are not entirely novel on their own, so the novelty comes from the synergy of the components in the context of subject-driven generation using a MM-DiT.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in achieving a compelling balance between subject fidelity, text alignment, and computational efficiency, all without training. This is a desirable outcome as it avoids the resource demands of tuning-based methods and addresses the detail loss often associated with zero-shot approaches. The potential for multi-subject generation also broadens its applicability. If the method performs well in practice, it could become a useful tool for personalized content creation. However, its dependence on external models during the collaging phase might be a limitation.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Training-free: Avoiding per-subject fine-tuning is a major strength, improving usability and efficiency.
    *   Pixel-level Detail Preservation: The paper convincingly argues and shows that the method retains fine details.
    *   Text Alignment: Text alignment is maintained, unlike some training-based approaches that can distort prompts.
    *   Multi-Subject Capability: The demonstrated ability to handle multiple reference subjects is a notable advantage.
    *   Comprehensive Evaluation: The quantitative evaluation using CLIP and ImageReward, alongside ablation studies, adds rigor.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Reliance on External Models: The framework depends on the performance of external models (GroundingDINO, SAM, LaMa) for collage construction. Failures in these modules can negatively impact the results. This dependence makes the process less end-to-end and introduces potential failure points.
    *   Limited Base Model Choice: Although using FLUX.1 gives state-of-the-art results, it requires very high GPU memory, making it accessible only for those with special hardware.
    *   Parameter Tuning: While the authors show that performance is affected by certain parameters, they have been able to get very good performance without fine-tuning the actual model. This parameter tuning could still limit applicability.

*   **Potential Influence:** The method has the potential to influence the direction of subject-driven generation by demonstrating that high fidelity and efficiency can be achieved without training. The proposed techniques could be adopted or adapted by other researchers.

*   **Overall Assessment:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field. While the components are not entirely revolutionary, the combination of techniques, particularly the feature grafting and noise initialization strategies, is novel and effective. The method has the potential to become a widely-used and practical approach for subject-driven generation if it can be made more efficient and less reliant on external models. The results are convincing, and the paper is well-written and clearly explains the methodology.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### From Human Memory to AI Memory: A Survey on Memory Mechanisms in the Era of LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.15965v2)
- **Authors**: Yaxiong Wu, Sheng Liang, Chen Zhang, Yichao Wang, Yongyue Zhang, Huifeng Guo, Ruiming Tang, Yong Liu
- **Abstract**: Memory is the process of encoding, storing, and retrieving information, allowing humans to retain experiences, knowledge, skills, and facts over time, and serving as the foundation for growth and effective interaction with the world. It plays a crucial role in shaping our identity, making decisions, learning from past experiences, building relationships, and adapting to changes. In the era of large language models (LLMs), memory refers to the ability of an AI system to retain, recall, and use information from past interactions to improve future responses and interactions. Although previous research and reviews have provided detailed descriptions of memory mechanisms, there is still a lack of a systematic review that summarizes and analyzes the relationship between the memory of LLM-driven AI systems and human memory, as well as how we can be inspired by human memory to construct more powerful memory systems. To achieve this, in this paper, we propose a comprehensive survey on the memory of LLM-driven AI systems. In particular, we first conduct a detailed analysis of the categories of human memory and relate them to the memory of AI systems. Second, we systematically organize existing memory-related work and propose a categorization method based on three dimensions (object, form, and time) and eight quadrants. Finally, we illustrate some open problems regarding the memory of current AI systems and outline possible future directions for memory in the era of large language models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper abstract and introduction:

**Summary:**

The paper provides a comprehensive survey of memory mechanisms in AI systems powered by Large Language Models (LLMs). It draws parallels between human memory and AI memory, categorizing AI memory based on three dimensions: object (personal vs. system), form (parametric vs. non-parametric), and time (short-term vs. long-term).  It organizes existing work related to memory in LLMs according to these dimensions, identifies open problems in current AI memory research, and suggests future research directions for memory development in the LLM era.  The goal is to inspire the development of more powerful and human-centric AI memory systems. The paper promises to comprehensively define LLM-driven AI systems' memory, propose a three-dimensional classification, analyze personal and system memory related work, and identify future research directions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its attempt to provide a unified framework for understanding memory in LLM-driven AI systems by drawing analogies with human memory and proposing a three-dimensional (object, form, time) classification scheme. Existing reviews often focus on the temporal aspect of memory (short-term vs. long-term) or specific techniques like RAG. The holistic approach and structured categorization are potentially valuable.

*   **Significance:**  The significance of the survey depends heavily on the depth of the analysis and the insights it provides. Simply categorizing existing work is not enough; the paper needs to synthesize the literature and identify genuine opportunities for advancement. If the paper can use the three-dimensional framework to highlight previously unappreciated connections between different memory techniques or to uncover fundamental limitations of current approaches, then it would be highly significant. The stated aims - to enhance personalized responses and improve complex task performance are both important goals in the field of AI agents.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The attempt to draw inspiration from human memory is a sound approach, as human memory systems are highly evolved and efficient.
    *   The three-dimensional categorization provides a structured way to analyze the diverse landscape of memory mechanisms in LLMs.
    *   Focusing on both personal and system memory aspects is relevant, as AI systems increasingly need to understand user context and perform complex reasoning.
    *   Identification of open problems and future directions is crucial for guiding future research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The abstract doesn't provide enough detail about the specific open problems identified or the proposed future directions.  The value hinges on the quality and specificity of these points.
    *   The three dimensions could potentially be too simplistic to capture the complexities of AI memory. Is "object" truly sufficient to define information retained? How does modality fit within this framework (multimodal data)?
    *   The success of the paper is contingent on the comprehensiveness and analytical depth of the literature review. If it is just a list of methods categorized into tables, its significance would be limited.
    *   The description of Memory Mechanisms (encoding, storage, retrieval, etc.) in the introduction, while accurate, might be well-known in the field, so the survey needs to go beyond basic definitions.

*   **Potential Influence:** If the survey is well-executed and provides valuable insights, it could:
    *   Serve as a valuable resource for researchers and practitioners working on memory-enhanced AI systems.
    *   Stimulate new research directions by highlighting key open problems and promising areas for future exploration.
    *   Help to bridge the gap between human cognitive science and AI, leading to more human-like and effective AI systems.

*   **Justification:** While the concept is promising, the novelty and significance are not high enough to warrant a high score due to lacking crucial information. A score in the lower-mid range is more appropriate until the actual contents of the paper is evaluated.

### MVQA: Mamba with Unified Sampling for Efficient Video Quality Assessment
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.16003v1)
- **Authors**: Yachun Mi, Yu Li, Weicheng Meng, Chaofeng Chen, Chen Hui, Shaohui Liu
- **Abstract**: The rapid growth of long-duration, high-definition videos has made efficient video quality assessment (VQA) a critical challenge. Existing research typically tackles this problem through two main strategies: reducing model parameters and resampling inputs. However, light-weight Convolution Neural Networks (CNN) and Transformers often struggle to balance efficiency with high performance due to the requirement of long-range modeling capabilities. Recently, the state-space model, particularly Mamba, has emerged as a promising alternative, offering linear complexity with respect to sequence length. Meanwhile, efficient VQA heavily depends on resampling long sequences to minimize computational costs, yet current resampling methods are often weak in preserving essential semantic information. In this work, we present MVQA, a Mamba-based model designed for efficient VQA along with a novel Unified Semantic and Distortion Sampling (USDS) approach. USDS combines semantic patch sampling from low-resolution videos and distortion patch sampling from original-resolution videos. The former captures semantically dense regions, while the latter retains critical distortion details. To prevent computation increase from dual inputs, we propose a fusion mechanism using pre-defined masks, enabling a unified sampling strategy that captures both semantic and quality information without additional computational burden. Experiments show that the proposed MVQA, equipped with USDS, achieve comparable performance to state-of-the-art methods while being $2\times$ as fast and requiring only $1/5$ GPU memory.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "MVQA: Mamba with Unified Sampling for Efficient Video Quality Assessment":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MVQA, a new video quality assessment (VQA) model built on the Mamba state-space architecture, designed for efficiency in handling long, high-resolution videos. The key innovation is the Unified Semantic and Distortion Sampling (USDS) strategy. USDS combines downsampled semantic information from the entire frame with high-resolution distortion detail captured using patch sampling. This dual approach aims to preserve both overall context and fine-grained quality defects.  The paper shows MVQA achieves comparable or better performance than existing methods (including CNNs and Transformers) with reduced computational costs, particularly memory usage and processing time. The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of MVQA and USDS through extensive experiments on several VQA datasets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper exhibits moderate novelty. The application of Mamba to VQA is a potentially significant contribution. State-space models like Mamba offer an attractive alternative to Transformers for long sequence modeling due to their linear complexity. The proposed USDS sampling strategy is also novel, addressing the limitations of both naive resampling methods (like resizing) and localized patch sampling. However, the idea of combining multi-resolution features isn't entirely new in VQA, but the specific combination of semantic and distortion sampling with masked fusion is a unique and sensible approach. The core innovation lies in the judicious integration of Mamba with the specifically designed USDS method.

* **Significance:** The potential significance of this work is considerable. Efficient VQA is critical for real-world applications dealing with massive video datasets. MVQA’s demonstrated efficiency gains (2x speedup and reduced memory usage) are impactful. By using Mamba and optimizing the sampling strategy, the paper addresses a key bottleneck in current deep learning-based VQA systems.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Efficiency:**  The paper clearly demonstrates the efficiency advantages of MVQA, both in terms of processing speed and memory footprint, which is a major focus.  The benchmarks comparing MVQA to FAST-VQA are compelling.
    * **USDS Strategy:** The USDS strategy is well-motivated. Combining semantic and distortion information makes intuitive sense for VQA.  The experiments support the effectiveness of this combined sampling approach.
    * **Comprehensive Experiments:**  The paper presents a range of experiments across multiple VQA datasets, which helps to validate the generalizability of the proposed approach. The Ablation Studies are especially important to demonstrate the effectiveness of different components of the framework.
    * **Clear Writing:** The method is described reasonably well and it's easy to understand the goals and approaches in the paper.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Incremental Advances:** While the paper contains multiple novel ideas, they are still incremental improvements over existing approaches.  The experiments show "comparable" or "better" results, but not a dramatic revolution in performance. A comparison of USDS against existing methods (like attention-based mechanisms) might be appropriate.
    * **Limited Ablation on Masking:** It would be beneficial to ablate the masking strategy further.  Exploring different masking ratios or adaptive masking strategies could improve performance.
    * **Mamba Implementation Details:** The details of the Vision Mamba encoder's architecture and hyperparameter settings are not sufficiently detailed. A more rigorous examination of architectural choices could improve the impact.

* **Impact:** The paper is likely to influence future research in efficient VQA and potentially inspire similar applications of Mamba and novel sampling strategies in other video processing tasks. The proposed combination of techniques provides a solid baseline for future research and development.

**Justification for Score:**

MVQA presents a solid contribution to the field of VQA with a reasonably strong combination of ideas. The Mamba architecture and USDS approach are both well-motivated and convincingly validated through empirical experiments. However, the limitations outlined above (incremental advances, ablation of masking, architectural details) prevent it from being a truly transformative work.
However, the ability to process longer sequences in real-time, at acceptable accuracy could have impact on video processing.

### CAPO: Cost-Aware Prompt Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.16005v2)
- **Authors**: Tom Zehle, Moritz Schlager, Timo Heiß, Matthias Feurer
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have revolutionized natural language processing by solving a wide range of tasks simply guided by a prompt. Yet their performance is highly sensitive to prompt formulation. While automated prompt optimization addresses this challenge by finding optimal prompts, current methods require a substantial number of LLM calls and input tokens, making prompt optimization expensive. We introduce CAPO (Cost-Aware Prompt Optimization), an algorithm that enhances prompt optimization efficiency by integrating AutoML techniques. CAPO is an evolutionary approach with LLMs as operators, incorporating racing to save evaluations and multi-objective optimization to balance performance with prompt length. It jointly optimizes instructions and few-shot examples while leveraging task descriptions for improved robustness. Our extensive experiments across diverse datasets and LLMs demonstrate that CAPO outperforms state-of-the-art discrete prompt optimization methods in 11/15 cases with improvements up to 21%p. Our algorithm achieves better performances already with smaller budgets, saves evaluations through racing, and decreases average prompt length via a length penalty, making it both cost-efficient and cost-aware. Even without few-shot examples, CAPO outperforms its competitors and generally remains robust to initial prompts. CAPO represents an important step toward making prompt optimization more powerful and accessible by improving cost-efficiency.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and a critical evaluation of the paper, along with a novelty and significance score:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Cost-Aware Prompt Optimization (CAPO), a novel algorithm for efficiently optimizing prompts for large language models (LLMs). CAPO integrates AutoML techniques, such as racing and multi-objective optimization, to reduce the number of LLM calls and the length of prompts, addressing the high cost of prompt optimization. The algorithm leverages LLMs as operators within an evolutionary framework, combining instruction and few-shot example optimization and incorporating task descriptions for improved robustness. Experiments across various datasets and LLMs demonstrate that CAPO outperforms state-of-the-art discrete prompt optimization methods in a majority of cases, offering significant improvements in performance while reducing costs. Ablation studies validate the effectiveness of the integrated techniques.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses a significant practical problem:** The paper tackles a crucial challenge in LLM utilization: the expense associated with prompt optimization.  This is very important as LLM usage costs rise.
    *   **Novel integration of techniques:** CAPO's integration of AutoML techniques (racing, multi-objective optimization) with an evolutionary prompt optimization framework is a novel approach. Combining these techniques gives real gains over the previous work.
    *   **Cost-efficiency:** CAPO's cost-aware design demonstrably reduces the number of LLM calls and input tokens required, making prompt optimization more accessible. The ablation studies convincingly show the impact of each component on cost and performance.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The paper features a thorough experimental evaluation across diverse datasets and LLMs, providing robust evidence for CAPO's effectiveness. Also, comprehensive ablation studies were conducted to show the efficacy of design choices.
    *   **Task description integration:** Leveraging task descriptions to improve robustness is a practical and effective strategy.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited novelty in individual components:**  While the *integration* is novel, the individual components (racing, evolutionary algorithms) are well-established. The core evolutionary algorithm is built on top of EvoPrompt, so the improvements are incremental.
    *   **Hyperparameter sensitivity:** Although a hyperparameter sensitivity analysis is presented, the optimal configuration of CAPO may still require task-specific tuning. The paper would be strengthened by guidelines on selecting appropriate hyperparameter values.
    *   **Limited to discrete prompts:** The algorithm focuses on discrete prompt optimization and does not consider continuous or "soft" prompts, which are also a viable option.
    *   **Evaluation datasets:** While the evaluation is broad, the reliance on somewhat older datasets is a potential weakness, as some LLMs might be trained on these datasets, skewing the results (although the authors acknowledge this).

*   **Significance and Impact:**

    *   CAPO represents a significant step towards making prompt optimization more practical and accessible. Reducing the cost of optimization is critical for wider adoption of LLMs.
    *   The algorithm's robustness to initial prompts makes it valuable for users without deep expertise in prompt engineering.
    *   The insights from the ablation studies offer valuable guidance for future research in prompt optimization.

*   **Areas for Improvement:**

    *   More specific guidelines for hyperparameter tuning.
    *   Comparisons against more recent/state-of-the-art prompt optimization methods that might not have been available at submission time.
    *   Exploration of combining CAPO with continuous prompt optimization techniques.
    *   Addressing concerns about dataset contamination more directly, potentially by curating a set of truly novel datasets.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper is a solid contribution that addresses a practical challenge and presents a novel and well-evaluated solution. While the individual components are not groundbreaking, the *integration* is significant, and the results demonstrate a clear advantage over existing methods. The thorough experimental design strengthens the paper's credibility. However, the incremental nature of the improvements, alongside the concerns related to dataset contamination and hyperparameter sensitivity, prevent it from receiving a higher score.

- **Score**: 7/10

### Efficient Temporal Consistency in Diffusion-Based Video Editing with Adaptor Modules: A Theoretical Framework
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.16016v1)
- **Authors**: Xinyuan Song, Yangfan He, Sida Li, Jianhui Wang, Hongyang He, Xinhang Yuan, Ruoyu Wang, Jiaqi Chen, Keqin Li, Kuan Lu, Menghao Huo, Binxu Li, Pei Liu
- **Abstract**: Adapter-based methods are commonly used to enhance model performance with minimal additional complexity, especially in video editing tasks that require frame-to-frame consistency. By inserting small, learnable modules into pretrained diffusion models, these adapters can maintain temporal coherence without extensive retraining. Approaches that incorporate prompt learning with both shared and frame-specific tokens are particularly effective in preserving continuity across frames at low training cost. In this work, we want to provide a general theoretical framework for adapters that maintain frame consistency in DDIM-based models under a temporal consistency loss. First, we prove that the temporal consistency objective is differentiable under bounded feature norms, and we establish a Lipschitz bound on its gradient. Second, we show that gradient descent on this objective decreases the loss monotonically and converges to a local minimum if the learning rate is within an appropriate range. Finally, we analyze the stability of modules in the DDIM inversion procedure, showing that the associated error remains controlled. These theoretical findings will reinforce the reliability of diffusion-based video editing methods that rely on adapter strategies and provide theoretical insights in video generation tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a theoretical framework for improving temporal consistency in diffusion-based video editing, specifically using adapter modules. It focuses on analyzing the temporal consistency loss and the DDIM (Denoising Diffusion Implicit Models) framework. The paper provides theoretical proofs regarding the differentiability and Lipschitz continuity of the temporal consistency loss, convergence properties of gradient descent optimization, and the stability of DDIM inversion with bilateral filtering.  It also analyzes the expressive power of shared and unshared prompt tokens in approximating feature representations. The paper validates these theoretical findings with empirical studies that show the effectiveness of UNet adapters in maintaining structural and semantic coherence across frames.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Rigorous Theoretical Analysis:** The paper provides a detailed mathematical analysis of key components used in diffusion-based video editing, particularly concerning temporal consistency. The proofs regarding differentiability, Lipschitz continuity, and convergence are valuable contributions.
    *   **Practical Relevance:** The theoretical findings directly relate to commonly used techniques in diffusion-based video editing, such as adapter modules, cosine similarity losses, and DDIM inversion. The analysis provides a strong theoretical foundation for these practical methods.
    *   **Comprehensive Analysis:** The paper covers several crucial aspects, including the temporal consistency loss, DDIM inversion stability, and the expressive power of prompt tokens. This holistic approach makes the work more impactful.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The empirical studies support the theoretical claims and demonstrate the practical benefits of adapter-based methods in improving temporal consistency. The visualizations and quantitative metrics strengthen the paper's conclusions.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Novelty in Concepts:** The techniques (adapter modules, cosine similarity loss, and DDIM) themselves are not entirely novel.  The *novelty* primarily lies in the theoretical *analysis* of these existing techniques. This must be accounted for.
    *   **Strong Assumptions:** Some of the theoretical results rely on strong assumptions (e.g., bounded feature norms, Lipschitz continuity, the full rank assumption). While the authors acknowledge these assumptions, the extent to which they hold in practice could limit the applicability of the results.
    *   **Complexity of Proofs:** Some of the mathematical proofs are quite involved, which may make the paper inaccessible to a broader audience. Simplification or more intuitive explanations of key results would be beneficial.
    *   **Incremental Contribution:** While providing a robust theoretical basis, the paper is more an analysis and justification of existing practices than a radical departure introducing fundamentally new algorithms or approaches.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Solid Theoretical Grounding:**  The paper contributes by providing a strong theoretical foundation for a set of techniques that have previously relied primarily on empirical observations. This enables the development of more principled video editing methods.
    *   **Guidance for Future Research:** The theoretical analysis provides insights into the factors that influence temporal consistency, which can guide the development of improved video editing algorithms.
    *   **Increased Trustworthiness:** Demonstrating the stability and convergence of these techniques increases trust in their utility.

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty is in providing the theoretical justification and analysis. It is *not* in proposing novel algorithms or architectures. The depth of theoretical analysis distinguishes this paper.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of 7/10.

*   The paper offers a valuable contribution by providing a rigorous theoretical understanding of adapter-based methods for temporal consistency in video editing. The mathematical proofs are well-executed and contribute to solidifying understanding in the field.
*   However, the paper's reliance on existing techniques and relatively strong assumptions, as well as the complexity of the proofs, limits its transformative impact.
*   While not groundbreaking in terms of novel algorithms, the paper contributes significantly by providing a solid theoretical foundation for existing and future work, particularly in designing adapter-based video-to-video generation techniques and analyses. The theoretical tools for this type of analysis have been underexplored, thus making this a key piece of work.

### PointLoRA: Low-Rank Adaptation with Token Selection for Point Cloud Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.16023v1)
- **Authors**: Song Wang, Xiaolu Liu, Lingdong Kong, Jianyun Xu, Chunyong Hu, Gongfan Fang, Wentong Li, Jianke Zhu, Xinchao Wang
- **Abstract**: Self-supervised representation learning for point cloud has demonstrated effectiveness in improving pre-trained model performance across diverse tasks. However, as pre-trained models grow in complexity, fully fine-tuning them for downstream applications demands substantial computational and storage resources. Parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) methods offer a promising solution to mitigate these resource requirements, yet most current approaches rely on complex adapter and prompt mechanisms that increase tunable parameters. In this paper, we propose PointLoRA, a simple yet effective method that combines low-rank adaptation (LoRA) with multi-scale token selection to efficiently fine-tune point cloud models. Our approach embeds LoRA layers within the most parameter-intensive components of point cloud transformers, reducing the need for tunable parameters while enhancing global feature capture. Additionally, multi-scale token selection extracts critical local information to serve as prompts for downstream fine-tuning, effectively complementing the global context captured by LoRA. The experimental results across various pre-trained models and three challenging public datasets demonstrate that our approach achieves competitive performance with only 3.43% of the trainable parameters, making it highly effective for resource-constrained applications. Source code is available at: https://github.com/songw-zju/PointLoRA.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the PointLoRA paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PointLoRA, a parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) method for point cloud learning. It combines low-rank adaptation (LoRA) with a novel multi-scale token selection mechanism. LoRA is applied to the most parameter-intensive layers of point cloud transformers to capture global features, while multi-scale token selection extracts crucial local information, acting as prompts for downstream fine-tuning. Experimental results on various datasets and pre-trained models demonstrate competitive performance with a significantly reduced number of trainable parameters (around 3.43%).

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The combination of LoRA with multi-scale token selection is novel in the context of point cloud processing. While LoRA is a well-established technique in NLP and computer vision, its application to point clouds, coupled with a tailored token selection strategy, represents a distinct contribution. The explicit connection to PointNet's architecture is also a valuable insight. However, the individual components (LoRA and token selection) are not fundamentally new in themselves; the novelty lies in their specific integration and application to this particular domain.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its potential to make point cloud learning more accessible and practical, especially for resource-constrained applications. By dramatically reducing the number of trainable parameters, PointLoRA addresses a critical challenge associated with deploying large pre-trained models in real-world scenarios. The improved efficiency in storage and computation can democratize access to state-of-the-art point cloud processing techniques. Furthermore, the consistent results across diverse datasets and models strengthen the claim that the improvement isn't coincidental to specific hyperparameter settings or training conditions.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Parameter Efficiency:** The most prominent strength is the achieved parameter reduction without a severe drop in performance. This opens up the use of large pre-trained models for devices and scenarios with limited memory and computational resources.
    *   **Multi-Scale Token Selection:**  The token selection strategy is well-motivated by the unique characteristics of 3D point clouds, effectively integrating local and global information.
    *   **Solid Experimental Results:** Extensive experiments across multiple datasets and pre-trained models convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of PointLoRA.  Ablation studies meticulously analyze the contribution of individual components.
    *   **Well-Written and Clear:** The paper is clearly written, and it presents its ideas in a logical progression.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Heuristic Token Selection:** The multi-scale token selection mechanism, while effective, relies on heuristic design choices (e.g., number of scales, sampling strategies). A more adaptive or learnable token selection process could potentially further improve performance and robustness.
    *   **Limited Theoretical Analysis:** While the paper provides some analysis relating LoRA to PointNet, a deeper theoretical understanding of the interaction between LoRA and the multi-scale token selection would be beneficial.  A more formal explanation of why particular scaling choices of FPS lead to better performance would also add to the depth of the paper.
    *   **Lack of Runtime Comparison:** The computational runtime comparison is missing to emphasize the efficiency of the method and parameter reduction (training and inference time).
    *   **Scalability to Extremely Large Models (Speculation):**  While the paper shows preliminary results scaling to a 360M parameter model, it doesn't conclusively address how well this will generalize to significantly larger models.

*   **Potential Impact:**  PointLoRA has the potential to significantly impact the field by enabling wider adoption of pre-trained point cloud models, especially in edge computing scenarios and other resource-constrained settings. By lowering the barrier to entry for advanced point cloud processing, it could facilitate innovation in various domains, including autonomous driving, robotics, and environmental monitoring.

**Justification of Score:**

Given the paper's novelty in combining LoRA with multi-scale token selection for point cloud processing, its substantial parameter efficiency gains, and its solid experimental validation, it represents a valuable contribution to the field. While the token selection mechanism could benefit from a more adaptive approach and more runtime benchmarks, and a deeper theoretical analysis is warranted, the paper's strengths outweigh its weaknesses.

### LiveCC: Learning Video LLM with Streaming Speech Transcription at Scale
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.16030v1)
- **Authors**: Joya Chen, Ziyun Zeng, Yiqi Lin, Wei Li, Zejun Ma, Mike Zheng Shou
- **Abstract**: Recent video large language models (Video LLMs) often depend on costly human annotations or proprietary model APIs (e.g., GPT-4o) to produce training data, which limits their training at scale. In this paper, we explore large-scale training for Video LLM with cheap automatic speech recognition (ASR) transcripts. Specifically, we propose a novel streaming training approach that densely interleaves the ASR words and video frames according to their timestamps. Compared to previous studies in vision-language representation with ASR, our method naturally fits the streaming characteristics of ASR, thus enabling the model to learn temporally-aligned, fine-grained vision-language modeling. To support the training algorithm, we introduce a data production pipeline to process YouTube videos and their closed captions (CC, same as ASR), resulting in Live-CC-5M dataset for pre-training and Live-WhisperX-526K dataset for high-quality supervised fine-tuning (SFT). Remarkably, even without SFT, the ASR-only pre-trained LiveCC-7B-Base model demonstrates competitive general video QA performance and exhibits a new capability in real-time video commentary. To evaluate this, we carefully design a new LiveSports-3K benchmark, using LLM-as-a-judge to measure the free-form commentary. Experiments show our final LiveCC-7B-Instruct model can surpass advanced 72B models (Qwen2.5-VL-72B-Instruct, LLaVA-Video-72B) in commentary quality even working in a real-time mode. Meanwhile, it achieves state-of-the-art results at the 7B/8B scale on popular video QA benchmarks such as VideoMME and OVOBench, demonstrating the broad generalizability of our approach. All resources of this paper have been released at https://showlab.github.io/livecc.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Live CC: Learning Video LLM with Streaming Speech Transcription at Scale" introduces a method for training Video Large Language Models (Video LLMs) using readily available Automatic Speech Recognition (ASR) transcripts from video closed captions.  The core idea is a novel "streaming training" approach that densely interleaves ASR words with corresponding video frames based on timestamps. This is designed to enable the model to learn fine-grained, temporally aligned vision-language relationships. To support this training, the authors create two datasets: Live-CC-5M (for pre-training) and Live-WhisperX-526K (for supervised fine-tuning). They also introduce a new benchmark, LiveSports-3K, to evaluate real-time video commentary generation using LLM-as-a-judge. Experiments show that their LiveCC-7B-Instruct model achieves competitive performance on video QA benchmarks and surpasses larger models in commentary quality, even in real-time.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The main novelty lies in the *streaming training* approach using ASR transcripts for video LLMs. This is a valuable departure from relying on expensive human annotations or proprietary model APIs. The careful curation of the datasets using active speaker detection and text perplexity filtering to improve ASR quality also showcases originality. The design of the LiveSports-3K benchmark, focusing on real-time commentary and leveraging LLM-as-a-judge, is also a novel contribution.
*   **Significance:** The paper is significant because it addresses a key bottleneck in video LLM training: the lack of scalable and cost-effective training data. By demonstrating that ASR transcripts can be effectively used for pre-training and fine-tuning, the authors open up new avenues for large-scale video LLM development. The ability to generate real-time video commentary, a capability not typically seen in general video LLMs, is also noteworthy. The performance of the LiveCC-7B-Instruct model on both general video QA and commentary tasks suggests a broader impact. The release of datasets and evaluation benchmarks further amplifies the paper's significance for the research community.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The "streaming training" approach is well-motivated and elegantly designed.
    *   The data curation pipeline is thorough and integrates cost-effective techniques to enhance ASR quality.
    *   The LiveSports-3K benchmark provides a valuable resource for evaluating real-time video commentary generation.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach, achieving state-of-the-art performance on multiple benchmarks.
    *   The resources (datasets, models) are publicly released, promoting reproducibility and future research.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The dependence on ASR transcripts might limit the model's ability to handle videos with poor audio quality or those lacking speech content.
    *   While the LLM-as-a-judge evaluation is innovative, it still relies on the judgment capabilities of another LLM (GPT-4o), which might introduce some bias.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more detailed analysis of the limitations of the LiveCC-7B-Instruct model, especially in edge cases or challenging scenarios.
    *   The evaluation could be more rigorous through direct comparison with state-of-the-art video captioning models, particularly concerning metrics focusing on temporal coherence and relevance to visual context.

**Justification:**

The paper provides a well-explained method to train video LLMs with cheap automatic speech recognition transcripts, along with newly introduced dataset and benchmark, all publicly released.
Therefore, the score is between 6 and 7.
