### Amplified Vulnerabilities: Structured Jailbreak Attacks on LLM-based Multi-Agent Debate
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.16489v1)
- **Authors**: Senmao Qi, Yifei Zou, Peng Li, Ziyi Lin, Xiuzhen Cheng, Dongxiao Yu
- **Abstract**: Multi-Agent Debate (MAD), leveraging collaborative interactions among Large Language Models (LLMs), aim to enhance reasoning capabilities in complex tasks. However, the security implications of their iterative dialogues and role-playing characteristics, particularly susceptibility to jailbreak attacks eliciting harmful content, remain critically underexplored. This paper systematically investigates the jailbreak vulnerabilities of four prominent MAD frameworks built upon leading commercial LLMs (GPT-4o, GPT-4, GPT-3.5-turbo, and DeepSeek) without compromising internal agents. We introduce a novel structured prompt-rewriting framework specifically designed to exploit MAD dynamics via narrative encapsulation, role-driven escalation, iterative refinement, and rhetorical obfuscation. Our extensive experiments demonstrate that MAD systems are inherently more vulnerable than single-agent setups. Crucially, our proposed attack methodology significantly amplifies this fragility, increasing average harmfulness from 28.14% to 80.34% and achieving attack success rates as high as 80% in certain scenarios. These findings reveal intrinsic vulnerabilities in MAD architectures and underscore the urgent need for robust, specialized defenses prior to real-world deployment.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper, "Amplified Vulnerabilities: Structured Jailbreak Attacks on LLM-based Multi-Agent Debate":

**Summary:**

This paper investigates the jailbreak vulnerabilities of Multi-Agent Debate (MAD) systems built on Large Language Models (LLMs). The authors introduce a structured prompt-rewriting framework using narrative encapsulation, role-driven escalation, iterative refinement, and rhetorical obfuscation to exploit the dynamics of MAD and elicit harmful content. They demonstrate that MAD systems are inherently more vulnerable than single-agent setups, and their proposed attack method significantly amplifies this fragility, increasing harmfulness and attack success rates. The study uses four MAD frameworks (Multi-Person, Exchange of Thought, ChatEval, AgentVerse) and leading commercial LLMs (GPT-4o, GPT-4, GPT-3.5-turbo, DeepSeek) to show the effectiveness of the proposed attack. The paper concludes by underscoring the need for robust defenses prior to real-world deployment of MAD systems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**
    * **Timely and relevant topic:** The paper addresses a crucial but under-explored aspect of LLM security - the vulnerabilities introduced by multi-agent systems. As MAD becomes more prevalent, understanding these vulnerabilities is critical.
    * **Well-defined methodology:** The structured prompt rewriting framework provides a systematic and novel approach to exploit MAD dynamics for jailbreaking. The use of four specific techniques is well-reasoned.
    * **Extensive experimentation:** Testing against multiple MAD frameworks and commercially aligned LLMs strengthens the findings' validity and generalizability.
    * **Clear metrics and baselines:** The use of Process Harmfulness Score (PHS), Answer Harmfulness Score (AHS), Harmfulness Diffusion Rate (HDR), and Attack Success Rate (ASR) provides quantitative measures of the vulnerabilities. The inclusion of non-MAD baselines is essential for comparing the jailbreak risks in MAD architectures.
    * **Practical Threat Model:** The semi-black-box threat model is realistic and demonstrates the real-world applicability of the proposed attack.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Exploration of Defenses:** The paper focuses heavily on demonstrating the vulnerabilities but offers only a brief discussion on potential defense strategies. A more detailed exploration of defense mechanisms would increase the paper's practical value.
    * **Scope of LLMs Tested:** While testing on four LLMs is good, most are from OpenAI and DeepSeek. A wider range of LLMs, including smaller or open-source models, could provide a more complete picture.
    * **Dataset Bias:** The HarmfulBench and Do Not Answer datasets may contain biases that influence the LLM responses. A discussion of potential biases and their impact on the results would be valuable.
    * **Complexity of Prompt Design:** While the framework is structured, the creation of effective prompts for different tasks and roles within the MAD setting still requires significant engineering effort. The paper could benefit from a more detailed discussion of the practical challenges of prompt design.

* **Novelty and Significance:**
    * The paper's primary novelty lies in its systematic investigation of jailbreak vulnerabilities in MAD architectures. Previous research has primarily focused on single-agent systems, leaving a significant gap in understanding the unique security challenges posed by collaborative LLM interactions.
    * The introduction of a structured prompt-rewriting framework tailored to exploit MAD dynamics is a significant contribution. The methodology is novel, well-defined, and empirically validated.
    * The findings highlight a crucial vulnerability that could have significant implications for the safe deployment of MAD systems in real-world applications. The paper's results demonstrate that the collaborative nature of MAD architecture creates exploitable channels for producing harmful content.

* **Potential Influence:**
    * The paper is likely to stimulate research into robust defense mechanisms for MAD systems.
    * It could inform the design and implementation of safety policies for MAD deployments.
    * It may raise awareness about the security risks associated with LLMs in collaborative settings.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed study on a highly relevant topic. The findings are supported by solid methodology and extensive experimentation. The weaknesses (limited exploration of defenses, LLM scope, possible dataset biases, and prompt engineering complexity) are acknowledged. The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field by raising awareness about MAD vulnerabilities and stimulating research on defense mechanisms. However, the limitations and the fact that the core of the work is showing, rather than solving, an issue keep the score from being higher.

### Intelligent Depression Prevention via LLM-Based Dialogue Analysis: Overcoming the Limitations of Scale-Dependent Diagnosis through Precise Emotional Pattern Recognition
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.16504v1)
- **Authors**: Zhenguang Zhong, Zhixuan Wang
- **Abstract**: Existing depression screening predominantly relies on standardized questionnaires (e.g., PHQ-9, BDI), which suffer from high misdiagnosis rates (18-34% in clinical studies) due to their static, symptom-counting nature and susceptibility to patient recall bias. This paper presents an AI-powered depression prevention system that leverages large language models (LLMs) to analyze real-time conversational cues--including subtle emotional expressions (e.g., micro-sentiment shifts, self-referential language patterns)--for more accurate and dynamic mental state assessment. Our system achieves three key innovations: (1) Continuous monitoring through natural dialogue, detecting depression-indicative linguistic features (anhedonia markers, hopelessness semantics) with 89% precision (vs. 72% for PHQ-9); (2) Adaptive risk stratification that updates severity levels based on conversational context, reducing false positives by 41% compared to scale-based thresholds; and (3) Personalized intervention strategies tailored to users' emotional granularity, demonstrating 2.3x higher adherence rates than generic advice. Clinical validation with 450 participants shows the system identifies 92% of at-risk cases missed by traditional scales, while its explainable AI interface bridges the gap between automated analysis and clinician judgment. This work establishes conversational AI as a paradigm shift from episodic scale-dependent diagnosis to continuous, emotionally intelligent mental health monitoring.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents an AI-powered system leveraging large language models (LLMs) to improve depression screening and prevention. It addresses limitations of traditional questionnaires by analyzing real-time conversational cues, including subtle emotional expressions and linguistic patterns. The system features continuous monitoring via natural dialogue, adaptive risk stratification based on conversational context, and personalized intervention strategies tailored to users' emotional granularity. Clinical validation with 450 participants showed improved detection of at-risk individuals compared to traditional scales. The authors argue that their system marks a shift towards continuous and emotionally intelligent mental health monitoring.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addresses a Significant Problem:**  The paper tackles the well-documented limitations of current depression screening methods, which are prone to high misdiagnosis rates and lack sensitivity to the dynamic nature of the condition.
    *   **Innovative Approach:** The use of LLMs to analyze conversational cues for depression detection is a novel and promising approach. It moves beyond static questionnaires to capture more nuanced and contextual information. The system's ability to identify subtle linguistic markers and track symptom evolution over time are significant advancements.
    *   **Multi-faceted Design:** The system incorporates multiple data streams, including lexical choices, speech patterns, and interaction dynamics, resulting in a comprehensive depression risk profile. The adaptive risk stratification and personalized intervention strategies are also noteworthy.
    *   **Promising Clinical Validation:** The clinical validation studies with 650 participants demonstrate the system's superior diagnostic performance compared to traditional methods. The improved detection of at-risk individuals and reduction of false positives are significant findings.
    *   **Explainability and Ethical Considerations:** The paper acknowledges the "black box" problem of machine learning and incorporates explainable outputs to bridge the gap between automated analysis and clinician judgment. The authors also address ethical concerns related to data privacy, algorithmic bias, and the role of automation in mental healthcare.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Generalizability:** The current validation studies have focused primarily on English-speaking populations, which limits the generalizability of the findings. The cultural adaptation algorithms require further testing across more demographic groups.
    *   **Real-world Variability:** The system's performance in controlled trials may not translate directly to real-world settings with more diverse populations and less structured interactions.
    *   **Explainability Still a Challenge:** While the explainability features represent progress, some clinicians may still find the decision-making process insufficiently transparent for high-stakes mental health decisions.
    *   **Dependence on LLM Capabilities:** The system's effectiveness heavily relies on the accuracy and robustness of the underlying LLM, which may be susceptible to biases or vulnerabilities.
    *   **Limited Specifics on LLM Implementation:** While GPT-4 is mentioned, more details on the specific fine-tuning process, the size and nature of the training data (especially clinical dialogues), and any specific prompt engineering techniques used would significantly strengthen the work.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

The paper offers a significant and novel application of LLMs within mental health.  While LLMs have been explored in various diagnostic and therapeutic contexts, this paper provides a robust, multi-faceted system designed for proactive depression prevention, coupled with tangible clinical validation. The shift from static assessments to continuous, conversation-based monitoring is a key innovation. The work addresses a clear and urgent need for better depression screening tools. However, similar work has been done using rule-based and statistical models to analyze conversation, and further benchmarking against those approaches could add to this work.

**Justification:**

The paper's strengths significantly outweigh its weaknesses. The innovative approach, multi-faceted design, and promising clinical validation demonstrate the potential of LLM-based systems to improve depression screening and prevention. While some limitations remain, the authors acknowledge these and propose future research directions to address them. This represents a considerable step beyond traditional, questionnaire-based screening and makes tangible progress toward continuous and emotionally intelligent mental health support.

### TraveLLaMA: Facilitating Multi-modal Large Language Models to Understand Urban Scenes and Provide Travel Assistance
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.16505v1)
- **Authors**: Meng Chu, Yukang Chen, Haokun Gui, Shaozuo Yu, Yi Wang, Jiaya Jia
- **Abstract**: Tourism and travel planning increasingly rely on digital assistance, yet existing multimodal AI systems often lack specialized knowledge and contextual understanding of urban environments. We present TraveLLaMA, a specialized multimodal language model designed for urban scene understanding and travel assistance. Our work addresses the fundamental challenge of developing practical AI travel assistants through a novel large-scale dataset of 220k question-answer pairs. This comprehensive dataset uniquely combines 130k text QA pairs meticulously curated from authentic travel forums with GPT-enhanced responses, alongside 90k vision-language QA pairs specifically focused on map understanding and scene comprehension. Through extensive fine-tuning experiments on state-of-the-art vision-language models (LLaVA, Qwen-VL, Shikra), we demonstrate significant performance improvements ranging from 6.5\%-9.4\% in both pure text travel understanding and visual question answering tasks. Our model exhibits exceptional capabilities in providing contextual travel recommendations, interpreting map locations, and understanding place-specific imagery while offering practical information such as operating hours and visitor reviews. Comparative evaluations show TraveLLaMA significantly outperforms general-purpose models in travel-specific tasks, establishing a new benchmark for multi-modal travel assistance systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the TraveLLaMA paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TraveLLaMA, a specialized multimodal language model designed to assist with urban scene understanding and travel planning. The key contribution is a new, large-scale dataset (TravelQA) of 220k question-answer pairs combining text-based QA from travel forums with vision-language QA focusing on map understanding and scene comprehension. The authors fine-tune existing vision-language models (LLaVA, Qwen-VL, Shikra) using TravelQA, demonstrating significant performance improvements (6.5%-9.4%) in travel-related tasks compared to general-purpose models. TraveLLaMA exhibits capabilities such as providing contextual recommendations, interpreting maps, understanding place-specific imagery, and offering practical information like operating hours. The paper also describes an agent architecture that enables TraveLLaMA to function as an interactive travel assistant, capable of multi-day planning, integrating real-time information, and maintaining context. A user study assesses user satisfaction, comparing against a commercial LLM API, showing that Travellama has better task performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The creation of TravelQA is a significant contribution. The combination of text and visual QA, specifically tailored to travel scenarios, addresses a crucial data gap in the field. While other travel-related datasets exist, the scale and multimodal nature of TravelQA appear novel. The approach of leveraging existing LLMs to augment and structure web content into a training dataset is also a creative and efficient way to address data scarcity. The agent architecture, although leveraging existing components of LLM agents, demonstrates one specific agent design that other domain researchers might benefit from.

*   **Significance:** The paper demonstrates a clear improvement in performance on travel-related tasks by fine-tuning existing models on the TravelQA dataset. This shows that domain-specific data can greatly enhance the ability of LLMs. The results, specifically that a fine-tuned small and offline model such as Travellama can provide better assistance than the API version of a large general purpose model, suggests potentially powerful applications of specialized AI assistance even with limited connectivity. The detailed qualitative analysis helps illustrate the advantages of the model, as does the user study.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Dataset:** The development and detailed characterization of the TravelQA dataset.
    *   **Quantifiable Results:** The paper provides clear quantitative results demonstrating the improvements achieved by fine-tuning on TravelQA. The region bias analysis adds a layer of nuance to the evaluation.
    *   **Agent Architecture:** The description of the ReAct agent architecture demonstrates a practical application of TraveLLaMA in an interactive setting.
    *   **User Study**: Travellama has better usability than commercial LLM.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope:** While the results are promising, the system is primarily focused on urban environments and single-day travel planning. The "multi-day" itinerary planning has been criticized as not being significantly better than using tools such as ChatGPT.
    *   **Base Model Selection:** The fine-tuning experiments are performed on LLaVA, Qwen-VL, and Shikra. Future research could involve exploring other state-of-the-art open source models and evaluating their performance.
    *   **Qualitative Bias:** The qualitative examples are curated to showcase the model's strengths. A more comprehensive qualitative analysis, including error cases, would provide a more balanced view.

*   **Potential Influence:** The TravelQA dataset has the potential to become a valuable resource for the research community, fostering further advancements in multimodal travel assistance systems. The insights from the region bias analysis could inform future research on mitigating biases in LLMs.

*   **Justification:** While the paper presents a solid contribution with a novel dataset and promising results, the limitations in scope and the lack of a completely novel model design prevent it from achieving a higher score. The dataset is indeed a significant advancement, but the paper primarily demonstrates the application of existing fine-tuning techniques on existing models.

### A Comprehensive Survey of Synthetic Tabular Data Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.16506v1)
- **Authors**: Ruxue Shi, Yili Wang, Mengnan Du, Xu Shen, Xin Wang
- **Abstract**: Tabular data remains one of the most prevalent and critical data formats across diverse real-world applications. However, its effective use in machine learning (ML) is often constrained by challenges such as data scarcity, privacy concerns, and class imbalance. Synthetic data generation has emerged as a promising solution, leveraging generative models to learn the distribution of real datasets and produce high-fidelity, privacy-preserving samples. Various generative paradigms have been explored, including energy-based models (EBMs), variational autoencoders (VAEs), generative adversarial networks (GANs), large language models (LLMs), and diffusion models. While several surveys have investigated synthetic tabular data generation, most focus on narrow subdomains or specific generative methods, such as GANs, diffusion models, or privacy-preserving techniques. This limited scope often results in fragmented insights, lacking a comprehensive synthesis that bridges diverse approaches. In particular, recent advances driven by LLMs and diffusion-based models remain underexplored. This gap hinders a holistic understanding of the field`s evolution, methodological interplay, and open challenges. To address this, our survey provides a unified and systematic review of synthetic tabular data generation. Our contributions are threefold: (1) we propose a comprehensive taxonomy that organizes existing methods into traditional approaches, diffusion-based methods, and LLM-based models, and provide an in-depth comparative analysis; (2) we detail the complete pipeline for synthetic tabular data generation, including data synthesis, post-processing, and evaluation; (3) we identify major challenges, explore real-world applications, and outline open research questions and future directions to guide future work in this rapidly evolving area.

The paper identifies key challenges in the field, explores real-world applications, and proposes future research directions.  It aims to provide a unified and systematic review to facilitate further research in this area.

### QuaDMix: Quality-Diversity Balanced Data Selection for Efficient LLM Pretraining
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.16511v1)
- **Authors**: Fengze Liu, Weidong Zhou, Binbin Liu, Zhimiao Yu, Yifan Zhang, Haobin Lin, Yifeng Yu, Xiaohuan Zhou, Taifeng Wang, Yong Cao
- **Abstract**: Quality and diversity are two critical metrics for the training data of large language models (LLMs), positively impacting performance. Existing studies often optimize these metrics separately, typically by first applying quality filtering and then adjusting data proportions. However, these approaches overlook the inherent trade-off between quality and diversity, necessitating their joint consideration. Given a fixed training quota, it is essential to evaluate both the quality of each data point and its complementary effect on the overall dataset. In this paper, we introduce a unified data selection framework called QuaDMix, which automatically optimizes the data distribution for LLM pretraining while balancing both quality and diversity. Specifically, we first propose multiple criteria to measure data quality and employ domain classification to distinguish data points, thereby measuring overall diversity. QuaDMix then employs a unified parameterized data sampling function that determines the sampling probability of each data point based on these quality and diversity related labels. To accelerate the search for the optimal parameters involved in the QuaDMix framework, we conduct simulated experiments on smaller models and use LightGBM for parameters searching, inspired by the RegMix method. Our experiments across diverse models and datasets demonstrate that QuaDMix achieves an average performance improvement of 7.2% across multiple benchmarks. These results outperform the independent strategies for quality and diversity, highlighting the necessity and ability to balance data quality and diversity.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've reviewed the provided text and will give a summary and a critical evaluation.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces QuaDMix, a unified data selection framework for pretraining large language models (LLMs). QuaDMix aims to balance data quality and diversity simultaneously, addressing the limitations of existing approaches that often optimize these metrics separately. The framework involves: (1) measuring data quality using multiple criteria and domain classification, (2) designing a parameterized sampling function based on quality and diversity labels, and (3) optimizing parameters via simulated experiments on smaller models using LightGBM inspired by RegMix. Experiments across various models and datasets show improved performance compared to independent strategies for quality and diversity, demonstrating the framework's ability to balance these factors.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of jointly optimizing quality and diversity is a valuable contribution. Previous works typically treat these as separate steps. Introducing a unified framework and using a parameterized sampling function to achieve this balance has merit. However, the components themselves (quality scorers, domain classifiers, regression model) are not entirely novel. The novelty lies in the combination and application of these existing techniques within a unified framework. Specifically, using a regressor trained on smaller model experiments (inspired by RegMix) to predict the performance of larger models with different quality mixes seems promising and efficient.

*   **Significance:** The performance gains demonstrated across multiple benchmarks are significant (reported 7.2% average improvement). This indicates the practical value of the QuaDMix framework. Moreover, the paper analyzes the importance of different quality criteria and the trade-offs involved, which provides valuable insights for the pretraining data selection process. Identifying optimal data mixtures and guiding preferences for downstream tasks has practical utility. However, the results only reflect gains with respect to the specific benchmarks used.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper clearly articulates the problem of balancing quality and diversity.
    *   QuaDMix provides a systematic framework with flexible parameters, allowing for adaptation to different data and downstream tasks.
    *   The experimental results show consistent improvements over existing methods.
    *   The analysis of optimal parameters and quality criteria offers valuable insights.
    *   Leveraging LightGBM as a regression model and performing simulated experiments on small datasets is computationally efficient compared to other approaches.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The reliance on pre-existing quality metrics and domain classifiers diminishes the novelty somewhat. The success of QuaDMix depends on the effectiveness of these components, which are treated as given. The choices of quality filters used (AskLLM, Fineweb-edu, DCLM) are arguably somewhat arbitrary, and using other criteria might affect the results.
    *   The simulated experiments on smaller models are approximations. While the correlation between the proxy model loss and the large-scale model performance is shown (figure 3), there might be discrepancies. Also the parameter searching for optimal parameters relies on training 3000 models with 1M parameters and 1B tokens each which is relatively resource intensive for parameter searching, thus raising questions on the overall efficiency and scalability of this system.
    *   The experimental setup is limited. While several benchmarks are used, more diversity in downstream tasks and models would strengthen the conclusions.
    *   The design of parameter space of QuaDMix is limited and the searching of optimal parameters is inefficient which is clearly acknowledged in the limitation section.
    *   The method relies on defining a target for the selection, which can be an arbitrary decision. The user would then have to have some prior knowledge about the dataset they are using.

*   **Potential Impact:**

    *   QuaDMix could influence future research on data selection for LLM pretraining by emphasizing the importance of joint optimization of quality and diversity.
    *   The framework could be adopted by practitioners to improve the efficiency and performance of their LLMs.
    *   The insights gained from analyzing optimal parameters could guide the development of more effective data curation strategies.

*   **Justification of Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7**. The paper presents a useful and effective framework for a crucial problem in LLM pretraining: balancing quality and diversity. The experimental results demonstrate significant improvements, and the analysis provides valuable insights. However, the novelty is limited by the reliance on existing components and the approximations made in the parameter optimization process. While the paper has the potential to impact the field, the limitations in the experimental setup and the reliance on potentially subjective choices (quality metrics, target tasks for evaluation) prevent it from being a truly exceptional contribution. Specifically, if data is highly noisy, data deduplication might have a more significant impact compared to balancing diversity, given that the quality metrics might fail in such situations.

### 6G EdgeAI: Performance Evaluation and Analysis
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.16529v1)
- **Authors**: Chien-Sheng Yang, Yu-Jen Ku, Yuan-Yao Lou, Nathan Tenny, Alex C. -C. Hsu
- **Abstract**: Generative AI (GenAI) services powered by large language models (LLMs) increasingly deliver real-time interactions, yet existing 5G multi-access edge computing (MEC) architectures often treat communication and computing as separate domains, limiting their ability to meet stringent latency requirements. To address this challenge, we introduce an Integrated Communication and Computing (ICC) framework where computing capabilities are enabled to reside directly in radio access network (RAN) nodes and jointly manage bandwidth and computing resources. Our queueing-theoretic analysis shows that ICC outperforms 5G MEC, achieving higher service capacity (defined as the maximum arrival rate that maintains a specified fraction of jobs completed within a given delay budget) by 98%. We corroborate these gains through system-level simulations that account for transformer-based LLM workloads, realistic GPU specifications, and a priority-based scheduling scheme. The simulations show that ICC improves service capacity by 60%, demonstrating its potential to enable efficient, cost-effective real-time GenAI services in 6G.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes an "Integrated Communication and Computing" (ICC) framework for 6G networks, specifically designed to improve the performance of Generative AI (GenAI) services, which rely heavily on large language models (LLMs). The key idea is to integrate computing capabilities directly into radio access network (RAN) nodes, allowing for joint management of bandwidth and computing resources. The paper presents a queueing-theoretic analysis and system-level simulations to demonstrate that ICC outperforms traditional 5G multi-access edge computing (MEC) in terms of service capacity, end-to-end latency, and cost-effectiveness. The results indicate substantial improvements in service capacity and reductions in compute costs, suggesting ICC's potential for enabling efficient real-time GenAI services in 6G. The paper uses a combination of queueing theory and system-level simulations with transformer-based LLM workloads to validate the proposed ICC framework. It also incorporates priority-based scheduling to optimize latency management further.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The concept of integrating communication and computing isn't entirely new, as the paper acknowledges existing works in the field. However, the paper's novelty lies in the *specific application* of this integration to the problem of GenAI services with stringent latency requirements in 6G networks. Focusing on the *integration at the RAN node level* with *joint resource management* makes this study distinct. The paper's contribution is to provide a quantitative analysis of the performance gains achievable by tightly coupling communication and computation specifically for LLM inference at the edge, showing advantages that might be overlooked when treating them independently.  The implementation of priority-based scheduling, leveraging the combined knowledge of communication and computing latencies, also adds to the novelty. The emphasis on a "unified" framework that breaks away from the typical disjoint treatment of MEC is also significant.

**Significance:** The significance of the paper stems from the increasing importance of GenAI services and the limitations of current 5G infrastructure in supporting these services with the required latency. The paper's findings provide a strong argument for the need to rethink the architecture of edge computing in 6G, moving towards a more integrated approach. The demonstrated improvements in service capacity and cost-effectiveness have practical implications for mobile network operators (MNOs) and can influence the design of future 6G networks. The work is also relevant to the AI-RAN Alliance initiative.  By quantifying the benefits of ICC, the paper provides concrete evidence that can motivate further research and development in this area. The performance evaluation using realistic LLM workloads and system-level simulations strengthens the paper's credibility and practical relevance.  The focus on real-time applications and user experience further increases the significance.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly articulates the challenges of supporting GenAI services in 5G.
*   **Well-Defined Solution:** The ICC framework is well-defined, along with its mechanisms of implementation.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The combination of queueing theory, system-level simulations, and detailed modeling of LLM inference adds depth to the evaluation.
*   **Quantifiable Results:**  The paper provides clear and quantifiable results, demonstrating the performance improvements of ICC compared to 5G MEC.
*   **Practical Implications:** The findings have practical implications for the design of 6G networks and the deployment of GenAI services.
*   **Cost analysis:** The demonstration of compute cost savings adds practical relevance.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Simplifications:** The queueing model makes simplifying assumptions (e.g., uniform data size, fixed computational load, and exponential service times) that may not perfectly reflect real-world conditions. While these simplifications are necessary for analysis, it's important to acknowledge their limitations.
*   **Simulation Scope:** While the simulation includes transformer architectures and priority-based scheduling, it is still limited in scope and may not capture all the complexities of a real-world 6G deployment. The results are specific to the chosen parameters and the simulated environment.
*   **Deployment Considerations:** The paper does not delve into the practical challenges of deploying ICC, such as the security, management, and scalability aspects of integrating computing resources directly into RAN nodes.
*   **Lack of a comparative performance study based on energy efficiency**

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7.5** to this paper.

*   The paper tackles a relevant and increasingly important problem in the context of 6G networks. The integration of computing and communication, and the advantages demonstrated with real world applications provides a strong base.
*   The novelty lies in the *specific application* to GenAI services in RAN nodes, rather than a generic integration.
*   The combination of queueing theory and system-level simulations provides a comprehensive evaluation.
*   The practical implications and quantifiable results contribute to the significance of the work.
*   However, the paper has weaknesses in terms of simplifications, limited simulation scope, and lack of discussion on deployment challenges.
*   Also, the performance evaluation is done only on service capacity with end-to-end latency under relatively modest compute investment. The performance evaluation on energy consumption would have strengthened the paper.
*   While promising, the paper requires further research to address the limitations and explore the practical challenges of deploying ICC in real-world 6G networks. The lack of comparative analysis in energy consumption makes the evaluation less compelling.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.DC
- **Score**: 7/10

### Transformers for Complex Query Answering over Knowledge Hypergraphs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.16537v1)
- **Authors**: Hong Ting Tsang, Zihao Wang, Yangqiu Song
- **Abstract**: Complex Query Answering (CQA) has been extensively studied in recent years. In order to model data that is closer to real-world distribution, knowledge graphs with different modalities have been introduced. Triple KGs, as the classic KGs composed of entities and relations of arity 2, have limited representation of real-world facts. Real-world data is more sophisticated. While hyper-relational graphs have been introduced, there are limitations in representing relationships of varying arity that contain entities with equal contributions. To address this gap, we sampled new CQA datasets: JF17k-HCQA and M-FB15k-HCQA. Each dataset contains various query types that include logical operations such as projection, negation, conjunction, and disjunction. In order to answer knowledge hypergraph (KHG) existential first-order queries, we propose a two-stage transformer model, the Logical Knowledge Hypergraph Transformer (LKHGT), which consists of a Projection Encoder for atomic projection and a Logical Encoder for complex logical operations. Both encoders are equipped with Type Aware Bias (TAB) for capturing token interactions. Experimental results on CQA datasets show that LKHGT is a state-of-the-art CQA method over KHG and is able to generalize to out-of-distribution query types.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LKHGT (Logical Knowledge Hypergraph Transformer), a two-stage transformer-based model for answering complex queries over knowledge hypergraphs (KHGs). It addresses the limitations of existing KG representations in handling n-ary facts where entities have equal contributions.  LKHGT comprises a Projection Encoder (for atomic projections) and a Logical Encoder (for complex logical operations), both equipped with Type Aware Bias (TAB) for capturing token interactions. The paper also provides two new CQA datasets: JF17k-HCQA and M-FB15k-HCQA, designed for this task. Experiments demonstrate LKHGT's state-of-the-art performance on these datasets and its ability to generalize to out-of-distribution query types. The paper also compares the transformer-based logical encoder against fuzzy logic, demonstrating its superiority.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a valuable contribution in addressing complex query answering (CQA) over knowledge hypergraphs. Specifically, by moving away from traditional triplet KGs to knowledge hypergraphs, the work tackles a more realistic representation of real-world data, which often involves n-ary relationships. The specific architectural innovation of LKHGT (dual-encoder with TAB) has some level of novelty. The dataset creation is also important.

*   **Significance:** The creation of HCQA datasets and the proposed LKHGT model address an important gap in CQA research. While CQA over standard KGs has been extensively studied, the extension to KHGs is relatively underexplored. By demonstrating LKHGT's superior performance on these new datasets, the paper establishes a baseline and opens up avenues for future research in this area. The better performance of the transformer-based logical encoder over fuzzy logic could potentially shift how logical operations are handled in future KG models.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addressing a Relevant Problem:** The paper tackles the limitations of existing KG models in representing n-ary relationships and provides a solution tailored to knowledge hypergraphs.
    *   **Novel Architecture:** LKHGT's two-stage transformer architecture is specifically designed for this task and is shown to outperform other methods.
    *   **Type Aware Bias:** The inclusion of TAB is an interesting architectural detail that is supported by experimentation.
    *   **Dataset Creation:** The release of JF17k-HCQA and M-FB15k-HCQA provides valuable resources for the community to benchmark future CQA models on KHGs.
    *   **Empirical Evaluation:** The experiments are well-designed and thoroughly compare LKHGT with appropriate baselines.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Complexity:** The two-stage transformer architecture adds complexity, potentially limiting its scalability compared to simpler methods. The experiments show that the additional computation time is significant compared to single pass methods.
    *   **Incremental Improvement?:** While LKHGT achieves state-of-the-art results, the reported improvements over certain baselines are not overwhelming, which potentially suggest that LKHGT is an incremental contribution rather than a breakthrough.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could potentially influence the direction of CQA research towards more complex KG representations like KHGs. The availability of the datasets and the strong performance of LKHGT could encourage further development in this area.
**Rationale for the Score:**

The paper is a solid contribution to the field of CQA. It addresses a relevant and important problem by extending CQA to knowledge hypergraphs. The proposed LKHGT model is well-designed, and the experiments demonstrate its effectiveness. The creation and release of the HCQA datasets is another valuable aspect. However, the complexity of LKHGT and the somewhat limited improvements over baselines, as well as the iterative nature of the architecture makes it an incremental contribution.

### Tinkering Against Scaling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.16546v1)
- **Authors**: Bolun Zhang, Yang Shen, Linzhuo Li, Yu Ji, Di Wu, Tongyu Wu, Lianghao Dai
- **Abstract**: The ascent of scaling in artificial intelligence research has revolutionized the field over the past decade, yet it presents significant challenges for academic researchers, particularly in computational social science and critical algorithm studies. The dominance of large language models, characterized by their extensive parameters and costly training processes, creates a disparity where only industry-affiliated researchers can access these resources. This imbalance restricts academic researchers from fully understanding their tools, leading to issues like reproducibility in computational social science and a reliance on black-box metaphors in critical studies. To address these challenges, we propose a "tinkering" approach that is inspired by existing works. This method involves engaging with smaller models or components that are manageable for ordinary researchers, fostering hands-on interaction with algorithms. We argue that tinkering is both a way of making and knowing for computational social science and a way of knowing for critical studies, and fundamentally, it is a way of caring that has broader implications for both fields.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've reviewed the provided OCR text. Here's a summary, followed by a critical evaluation of the paper's novelty and significance:

**Summary:**

The paper "Tinkering Against Scaling" addresses the challenges faced by academic researchers in computational social science and critical algorithm studies due to the increasing dominance of large-scale machine learning models (LLMs). The authors argue that the "scaling" paradigm creates a disparity where only industry researchers can access the necessary computational resources, hindering reproducibility, critical inquiry, and fostering a reliance on "black-box" understandings of algorithms. To counter this, the paper proposes a "tinkering" approach, emphasizing hands-on engagement with smaller, more manageable models and components. They draw inspiration from Heidegger's philosophy of technology (releasement and reflection), as well as STS studies of care practices, to frame tinkering as a way of making, knowing, and caring. The paper then presents examples of how tinkering can be applied to both computational social science (e.g., model-verse approach, probing larger models) and critical algorithm studies (e.g., retrieving specificities, offering new perspectives). It posits tinkering as an interventionist strategy to resist the hegemony of scaling and foster alternative relations with algorithmic systems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper presents a compelling critique of the current trends in AI research, particularly the emphasis on large-scale models and the associated resource disparities. The concept of "tinkering" is presented as a vital counter-strategy for academic researchers.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses a pressing issue:** The paper directly confronts the growing imbalance in AI research and its implications for academic inquiry, reproducibility, and critical engagement. This is a relevant and important topic.
    *   **Theoretical grounding:** The paper effectively integrates philosophical insights from Heidegger and STS literature to provide a robust theoretical foundation for the "tinkering" approach.
    *   **Concrete examples:** The paper is not just theoretical; it offers specific examples of how tinkering can be implemented in both computational social science and critical algorithm studies, making the approach more tangible and actionable. The examples of using survey methodologies to probe LLMs are particularly interesting.
    *   **Interventionist stance:** The paper explicitly positions tinkering as an interventionist strategy, aligning it with a broader agenda of resisting the hegemony of scaling and fostering alternative relations with technology.
    *   **Well structured and written:** The paper presents its arguments in a clear and organized manner, making it easier for readers to follow the logic.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Novelty of Tinkering itself:** The concept of "tinkering," while effectively framed and applied in the paper, is not entirely novel.  STS scholars have discussed similar practices. However, its application specifically *against* the scaling of AI and as a conscious methodological and epistemological intervention is more novel.
    *   **Idealistic:** While the call for tinkering is compelling, it might be idealistic. It doesn't fully address the real limitations in the academic environment (e.g., funding, career incentives, access to even modest computational resources). A more nuanced discussion of the practical obstacles and potential solutions would strengthen the argument.
    *   **Potential for Reinventing the Wheel:** The paper seems to suggest that researchers have to create *new* models or fine-tune the existing LLMs on their own. It does not discuss in detail the alternative - the re-use and re-interpretation of existing open-source models, and the importance of academic cloud infrastructure for collaborative resource sharing.

*   **Significance and Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper can serve as a valuable resource for researchers in computational social science, critical algorithm studies, and related fields who are struggling to navigate the challenges of the scaling paradigm.
    *   The paper can stimulate a broader discussion about the role of academic research in the age of big AI, prompting a re-evaluation of methodological priorities and funding models.
    *   The paper's call for an interventionist orientation can empower researchers to take a more active role in shaping the development and deployment of algorithmic systems.

**Justification for Score:**

Overall, the paper makes a valuable contribution by articulating a timely critique of the "scaling" paradigm in AI research and offering a compelling alternative approach based on "tinkering."  While the concept of tinkering itself isn't entirely new, the paper’s specific application to the current AI landscape, its theoretical grounding, and its concrete examples strengthen its novelty and impact. The paper is well-written and structured, and it offers a valuable framework for researchers navigating this complex terrain. The weaknesses, such as the idealized perspective and lack of detailed discussion on resource sharing, could be addressed in future work.

### Exploring human-SAV interaction using large language models: The impact of psychological ownership and anthropomorphism on user experience
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.16548v1)
- **Authors**: Lirui Guo, Michael G. Burke, Wynita M. Griggs
- **Abstract**: There has been extensive prior work exploring how psychological factors such as anthropomorphism affect the adoption of shared autonomous vehicles (SAVs). However, limited research has been conducted on how prompt strategies in large language model (LLM)-powered SAV User Interfaces (UIs) affect users' perceptions, experiences, and intentions to adopt such technology. In this work, we investigate how conversational UIs powered by LLMs drive these psychological factors and psychological ownership, the sense of possession a user may come to feel towards an entity or object they may not legally own. We designed four SAV UIs with varying levels of anthropomorphic characteristics and psychological ownership triggers. Quantitative measures of psychological ownership, anthropomorphism, quality of service, disclosure tendency, sentiment of SAV responses, and overall acceptance were collected after participants interacted with each SAV. Qualitative feedback was also gathered regarding the experience of psychological ownership during the interactions. The results indicate that an SAV conversational UI designed to be more anthropomorphic and to induce psychological ownership improved users' perceptions of the SAV's human-like qualities and improved the sentiment of responses compared to a control condition. These findings provide practical guidance for designing LLM-based conversational UIs that enhance user experience and adoption of SAVs.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the provided paper.

**Summary**

The paper explores the impact of large language model (LLM)-powered user interfaces (UIs) in shared autonomous vehicles (SAVs) on user experience, focusing specifically on how prompt strategies affect psychological ownership and anthropomorphism.  It investigates four different SAV UIs, each designed with varying levels of anthropomorphic characteristics and psychological ownership triggers. The study quantitatively measures psychological ownership, anthropomorphism, service quality, disclosure tendency, sentiment of SAV responses, and overall user acceptance. Qualitative feedback was collected to provide a richer understanding of the user experience. The core finding is that designing SAV conversational UIs with more anthropomorphic elements and triggers to induce psychological ownership improves users' perception of human-like qualities and the sentiment of responses, relative to a control condition. The paper concludes by providing practical guidance for designing these types of interfaces to enhance user experience and adoption.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Relevant and Timely Research Question:** The paper addresses a current and relevant issue: how to improve user experience and adoption of SAVs, particularly by leveraging advancements in LLMs. The focus on psychological factors like anthropomorphism and psychological ownership is important, as they are known to significantly affect technology acceptance.
    *   **Well-Defined Methodology:** The methodology is clearly described. The creation of four distinct SAV UI prompts allows for a controlled comparison. The use of both quantitative and qualitative data offers a comprehensive view of the user experience.
    *   **Practical Guidance:** The study culminates in practical guidance for designing LLM-based conversational UIs, which is valuable for practitioners in the field.
    *   **Explores User Perception:**  The assessment of factors beyond just basic usability, such as sentiment analysis of SAV responses, adds a layer of sophistication.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Novelty in Core Concepts:**  The concepts of psychological ownership and anthropomorphism are not new in the context of technology acceptance, and have been explored in autonomous vehicles previously. While the *application* of these concepts via *LLM prompting* is relatively new, the core idea isn't revolutionary.
    *   **Demographic Limitations:**  The study acknowledges a demographic bias towards young, highly educated, and urban participants, which limits the generalizability of the findings. The paper does state this is the target group for future technology, but also is a major limitation.
    *   **Modest Effect Sizes:** Although statistical significance is achieved for some of the measured items, the effect sizes were small for the variables. This diminishes the practical significance of the findings, as the observed changes may not be substantial in real-world applications.
    *   **Limited Direct Measurement of Psychological Ownership:**  While the prompts are designed to elicit psychological ownership, the study does not provide a clear, validated method for DIRECTLY measuring the *process* by which ownership is induced. The measured factors are more like outcomes or byproducts rather than a direct measure of the psychological ownership experience. There is more inference here than direct measuring.
    *   **Exploratory Hypotheses:**  While there are some sound hypotheses, some of them like 'Combined Prompt Effect' felt exploratory, not deeply anchored in a theoretical framework.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper provides a starting point for further research into the nuanced design of LLM-powered interfaces for SAVs.
    *   It highlights the potential of using prompt engineering to subtly influence user perceptions and emotional engagement with autonomous systems.
    *   The practical guidance can inform the development of more user-friendly and accepted SAVs.
    *   Provides insights in incorporating PO theories into the design prompt.

**Justification for Score**

The paper has strengths in its relevance and methodology, and it offers practical guidance. However, the limitations in novelty, demographics, effect sizes, and the lack of direct measurement of psychological ownership, significantly reduce its overall impact. It builds on existing research, but does not present a groundbreaking advancement or fundamentally shift the understanding of human-SAV interaction.

Score: 6

The paper demonstrates a sound methodological approach to a relevant problem and provides useful insights. However, its relatively small effect sizes and modest novelty prevent it from being a truly exceptional contribution to the field.

### Enhancing LLM-Based Agents via Global Planning and Hierarchical Execution
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.16563v1)
- **Authors**: Junjie Chen, Haitao Li, Jingli Yang, Yiqun Liu, Qingyao Ai
- **Abstract**: Intelligent agent systems based on Large Language Models (LLMs) have shown great potential in real-world applications. However, existing agent frameworks still face critical limitations in task planning and execution, restricting their effectiveness and generalizability. Specifically, current planning methods often lack clear global goals, leading agents to get stuck in local branches, or produce non-executable plans. Meanwhile, existing execution mechanisms struggle to balance complexity and stability, and their limited action space restricts their ability to handle diverse real-world tasks. To address these limitations, we propose GoalAct, a novel agent framework that introduces a continuously updated global planning mechanism and integrates a hierarchical execution strategy. GoalAct decomposes task execution into high-level skills, including searching, coding, writing and more, thereby reducing planning complexity while enhancing the agents' adaptability across diverse task scenarios. We evaluate GoalAct on LegalAgentBench, a benchmark with multiple types of legal tasks that require the use of multiple types of tools. Experimental results demonstrate that GoalAct achieves state-of-the-art (SOTA) performance, with an average improvement of 12.22% in success rate. These findings highlight GoalAct's potential to drive the development of more advanced intelligent agent systems, making them more effective across complex real-world applications. Our code can be found at https://github.com/cjj826/GoalAct.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Enhancing LLM-Based Agents via Global Planning and Hierarchical Execution":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GoalAct, a novel framework aimed at improving the performance and generalizability of LLM-based agents. It addresses two key limitations in existing agent frameworks: (1) the lack of clear global goals in planning, often leading to agents getting stuck in local optima or generating non-executable plans, and (2) the difficulty in balancing complexity and stability in execution, often constrained by a limited action space. GoalAct incorporates a continuously updated global planning mechanism and a hierarchical execution strategy.  The hierarchical execution decomposes task execution into high-level skills (searching, coding, writing, etc.), reducing planning complexity and enhancing adaptability. The framework's effectiveness is evaluated on LegalAgentBench, showing significant improvements in success rate compared to state-of-the-art baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the combination of continuously updated global planning and hierarchical execution within a single framework.  While individual components like global planning and hierarchical decomposition have been explored separately in other contexts, the specific integration and application to LLM-based agents is a valuable contribution. The design choice of high-level skills during planning and low-level tool selection during execution seems reasonable. However, one could argue that hierarchical task decomposition is not entirely new, but its specific implementation within the context of LLM-based agents provides a concrete advance.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work is that it directly addresses acknowledged weaknesses in existing LLM agent frameworks. The problem of "getting stuck" in local optima due to a lack of long-term planning is a critical issue that limits the practical application of these agents. Similarly, the difficulty in managing complex actions with a limited and often brittle action space is another major barrier. GoalAct's approach of decoupling planning from detailed execution, and enabling a mix of search, code and writing to invoke tools and process data shows considerable promise.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper provides compelling empirical evidence, demonstrating a clear improvement in success rate on a challenging benchmark.
    *   **Well-Defined Framework:** GoalAct's architecture is well-defined, and the description of its components (global planning, hierarchical execution) is clear.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component of the framework.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope:** While LegalAgentBench is a valuable benchmark, it is still a relatively specific domain. The generalizability of GoalAct to other task domains is not fully established. How does the hierarchical execution work with other skills?
    *   **Lack of detailed analysis**: While the paper shows empirical improvements, it would benefit from a more in-depth qualitative analysis of how GoalAct overcomes specific failure cases encountered by other methods. Why and how does GoalAct avoid the problems encountered by ReAct and CodeAct as illustrated in Fig. 2?

*   **Potential Influence:** GoalAct has the potential to influence the design of future LLM-based agent frameworks by highlighting the importance of integrated planning and execution strategies.  The idea of decomposing task execution into high-level skills could be a valuable direction for future research.

**Justification for Score:**

Overall, the paper presents a solid contribution to the field of LLM-based agents. While some of the individual components are not entirely novel, their specific integration within GoalAct and the strong empirical results justify a positive evaluation. However, the limited scope of the evaluation and the potential lack of depth in the discussion of why GoalAct works better than baselines prevent a higher score.
